- **B002** gecenin karartıp örtmesi — gecenin kararıp üzerini örtmesi · gecenin koyu karanlığı ve nesneleri örtmesi
  جنان الليل سواده وستره الأشياء (maqayis)؛ أجنه الليل وجن عليه الليل إذا أظلم حتى يستره بظلمته (ayn)؛ جن عليه الليل يجن بالضم جنونا (sihah)؛ جن عليه الليل وأجنه الليل إذا أظلم حتى يستره بظلمته (tahdhib)؛ جنه الليل وأجنه وجن عليه (mufradat)
- **B003** zemini ağaçlarla örtülü bahçe — zemini ağaçlarla örtülü bahçe veya koruluk
  الجنة البستان (maqayis;sihah)؛ الجنة الحديقة وهي بستان ذات شجر ونزهة (ayn)؛ العرب تسمي النخيل جنة (sihah)؛ كل بستان ذي شجر يستر بأشجاره الأرض (mufradat)
- **B004** ölüm sonrası gizli nimetler yurdu — ölüm sonrası ödül ve gizli nimetler yurdu
  الجنة ما يصير إليه المسلمون في الآخرة وهو ثواب مستور عنهم اليوم (maqayis)؛ سميت الجنة إما تشبيها بالجنة في الأرض وإما لستره نعمها عنا (mufradat)
- **B005** gözle görülmeyen ruhani varlıklar topluluğu — gözle görülmeyen ruhani varlıklar · görünmeyen varlıkların atası veya bir bireyi · görünmeyen ruhani varlıkların topluluğu · görünmeyen ruhani varlıkların çok bulunduğu yer
  الجن سموا بذلك لأنهم متسترون عن أعين الخلق (maqayis)؛ الجن جماعة ولد الجان وجمعهم الجنة والجنان (ayn;tahdhib)؛ الجن خلاف الإنس والواحد جني (sihah)؛ الجنة جماعة الجن (mufradat)؛ أرض مجنة كثيرة الجن (ayn;sihah;tahdhib)
- **B006** aklı örten akıl yitimi — aklını yitirmek; aklını yitirmiş duruma getirmek · akıl yitimi; benlik ile akıl arasındaki engel · aklını yitirmiş gibi davranmak
  الجنة الجنون وذلك أنه يغطي العقل (maqayis)؛ المجنة الجنون وجن الرجل وأجنه الله فهو مجنون (ayn)؛ جن الرجل جنونا وأجنه الله فهو مجنون (sihah)؛ به جنون وجنة ومجنة (tahdhib)؛ الجنون حائل بين النفس والعقل (mufradat)
- **B007** ana rahmindeki doğmamış çocuk — ana rahmindeki doğmamış çocuk · rahminde çocuk taşımak; çocuğun rahimde saklı kalması
  الجنين الولد في بطن أمه (maqayis)؛ أجنت الحامل الجنين أي الولد في بطنها (ayn)؛ الجنين الولد ما دام في البطن (sihah)؛ الجنين الولد في الرحم (tahdhib)؛ الجنين الولد ما دام في بطن أمه (mufradat)
- **B008** koruyucu siper veya savaş donanımı — koruyucu örtü, siper veya savaş donanımı · kalkan
  المجن الترس وكل ما استتر به من السلاح فهو جنة (maqayis)؛ المجن الترس والجنة الدرع وكل ما وقاك فهو جنتك (ayn)؛ الجنة ما استترت به من سلاح والجنة السترة والمجن الترس (sihah)؛ المجن الترس (tahdhib)؛ المجن والمجنة الترس الذي يجن صاحبه (mufradat)
- **B009** ölüyü örtüp gömme — ölüyü örtmek ve gömmek · gömüt; ölü örtüsü; gömülmüş kişi
  الجنين المقبور (maqayis)؛ الجنن القبر وقيل للكفن أيضا (ayn)؛ جننت الميت وأجننته أي واريته والجنن القبر (sihah)؛ جننته في القبر وأجننته والجنن القبر والجنن الكفن (tahdhib)؛ الجنين القبر (mufradat)
- **B010** duyulardan saklı yürek ve gizli yön — yürek veya yüreğin saklı iç yönü · gizli iş veya görünmeyen yön
  الجنان القلب (maqayis)؛ الجنان روع القلب (ayn;tahdhib)؛ أراد بالجن القلب (sihah)؛ الجنان القلب لكونه مستورا عن الحاسة (mufradat)؛ الجنان الأمر الخفي (tahdhib)
- **B011** bitkinin güçlenip boylanması ve sıklaşması — bitkinin güçlenmesi, boylanması, sıklaşması veya çiçek açması · uzun ağaç; bol otlu ve henüz otlanmamış arazi
  جن النبت جنونا إذا اشتد وخرج زهره (maqayis)؛ جن النبت جنونا أي طال والتف وخرج زهره ونخلة مجنونة أي طويلة (sihah)؛ للنبت الملتف الكثيف مجنون وجنت الرياض جنونا إذا اعتم نبتها (tahdhib)؛ جن التلاع والآفاق أي كثر عشبها (mufradat)
- **B012** yılan, özellikle beyaz bir tür — yılan, beyaz yılan veya belirli bir yılan türü
  الحية الذي يسمى الجان فهو تشبيه له بالواحد من الجان (maqayis)؛ الجان حية بيضاء (ayn)؛ الجان أيضا حية بيضاء (sihah)؛ الجان الحية وجمعها جوان (tahdhib)؛ الجان ضرب من الحيات (mufradat)
- **B013** halkın büyük kitlesi — insanların çoğunluğu veya halkın büyük kitlesi
  جنان الناس معظمهم ويسمى السواد (maqayis)؛ جنان الناس دهماؤهم (sihah)؛ جنانهم جماعتهم وسوادهم (tahdhib)
- **B014** bir şeyin ilk ve yeni dönemi — gençliğin, çocukluğun veya bir dönemin ilk başlangıcı
  كان ذلك في جن شبابه أي في أول شبابه (sihah)؛ كان ذلك في جن صباه أي في حداثته وكذلك جن كل شيء أول ابتدائه (tahdhib)
- **B015** uçuş sırasında çoğalan sinek vızıltısı — sineğin vızıltısının veya sesinin çoğalması · böcekse uçuş vızıltısının artması; bitkiyse sıklaşıp dolaşması
  جن الذباب أي كثر صوته (sihah)؛ جن الخازباز به جنونا يحتمل هذين الوجهين (sihah)؛ قيل هو ذباب وجنونه كثرة ترنمه في طيرانه وقيل هو نبت وجنون النبت التفافه (tahdhib)
- **B016** göğüs kemikleri ve kaburga uçları — göğüs kemikleri veya kaburgaların göğse yakın uçları
  الجناجن عظام الصدر (maqayis)؛ الجنجن والجناجن أطراف الأضلاع مما يلي الصدر وعظم القلب (ayn)؛ الجناجن عظام الصدر الواحد جنجن (sihah)
- **B017** içine girilip saklanılan yer — saklanılan yer; ayrıca kaynakta belirli bir eski pazar yerinin adı
  المجنة اسم موضع على أميال من مكة؛ كانت مجنة وذو المجاز وعكاظ أسواقا في الجاهلية؛ المجنة أيضا الموضع الذي يستتر فيه (sihah)



===== _commentary/v16/work/s089/surah.r2/channels.md =====
(source: latent_activation/network/v3/reviews/s089/reader_a_pilot.md)

# S089 Semantic Channel Discovery

## Parent Channels

### 1. Measured Cycles and Thresholds
- Semantic invariant: A continuum becomes intelligible when it is opened, counted, paired, or brought to a due boundary.
- Surface relation: direct; 89:1-5 anchors dawn, ten nights, pair and odd, night passage, and oath.
- Surprising reach: The temporal sequence extends into calendars, scheduled watches, ordinal days, and lunar wayfinding.

#### Subchannel A. Dawn Opening Through Night
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Night covers the field, movement continues within it, and dawn ruptures the cover as an opening.
- Active motifs: night-covering `ج ن ن:B002/m01`; night `ل ي ل:B001/m01`; night travel `س ر ي:B001/m01`; dawn `ف ج ر:B002/m01`; opening at dawn `ن ف س:B009/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:1 (الفجر); 89:2, 89:4 (ليال، الليل); 89:4 (يسر); 89:27 (النفس)
- Synthesis: The scene is a continuous passage from concealment to disclosure. The latent breath-opening sense of `ن ف س` makes dawn resemble a constricted field releasing into light.

#### Subchannel B. Watches, Intervals, and Due Time
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Time is divided into night watches and intervals until an appointed stage becomes due.
- Active motifs: night hours `ء ن ي:B002/m01`; interval spacing `و ت ر:B004/m01`; due maturity `ء ن ي:B003/m01`; appointed time `ج م م:B007/m01`; current night `ل ي ل:B003/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:23 (أنى); 89:3 (الوتر); 89:20 (جما); 89:2, 89:4 (ليل)
- Synthesis: These motifs form a schedule rather than a generic theme of time: intervals partition the night and culminate in a due moment.

#### Subchannel C. Tens, Fractions, and Recurring Days
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A counted whole is organized as ten, a tenth, a batch, an ordinal day, or a recurring festival.
- Active motifs: ten `ع ش ر:B001/m01`; tenth share `ع ش ر:B003/m01`; batch of ten `ع ش ر:B005/m01`; first or Sunday `ء ح د:B004/m01`; recurring holiday `ع و د:B007/m01`; tenth-day observance `ع ش ر:B011/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:2 (عشر); 89:25-26 (أحد); 89:6 (عاد)
- Synthesis: Counting becomes calendrical organization. The same lexical field moves from cardinal quantity to fractional share, ordinal day, and periodically returning observance.

#### Subchannel D. Pair, Odd, and the Unpaired Individual
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Units are joined into pairs while an odd remainder or solitary individual remains unpaired.
- Active motifs: joining into a pair `ش ف ع:B001/m01`; odd remainder `و ت ر:B001/m01`; individual unit `ء ح د:B005/m01`; singular isolation `ي ت م:B002/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:3 (الشفع والوتر); 89:25-26 (أحد); 89:17 (اليتيم)
- Synthesis: The direct pair-and-odd contrast expands into a social and numerical scene of joining versus solitary remainder.

#### Subchannel E. Endpoint and Culmination
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Motion or duration reaches an endpoint at the moment when completion is due.
- Active motifs: endpoint `ء ل ي:B001/m01`; due completion `ء ن ي:B003/m01`; appointed time `ج م م:B007/m01`; completed year `د ك ك:B007/m01`; terminal limit `ق د ر:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:28 (إلى); 89:23 (أنى); 89:20 (جما); 89:21 (دكا); 89:16 (قدر)
- Synthesis: The scene links direction, timing, and measure: an approach terminates when its allotted span has matured.

#### Subchannel F. Lunar Stations and Night Orientation
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Recurrent celestial shapes mark stations by which night can be divided and traversed.
- Active motifs: lunar mansion `ب ل د:B004/m01`; ostrich-like lunar station `ن ع م:B007/m02`; moon halo `ح ج ر:B006/m02`; night `ل ي ل:B001/m01`; forward guide `م ل ك:B008/m02`
- Ayah anchors: 89:8, 89:11 (البلاد); 89:15 (نعمه); 89:5 (حجر); 89:2, 89:4 (ليل); 89:22 (الملك)
- Synthesis: Shape analogy turns a lunar station, halo, and forward guide into a practical night-orientation scene.

### 2. Assembly, Alignment, and Dispersal
- Semantic invariant: Separate units change state by collecting, aligning, mixing, or scattering.
- Surface relation: direct; 89:3, 89:19-22 supply pair/odd, gathered consumption, abundance, repeated crushing, and rows.
- Surprising reach: The same transformations organize crowds, heaps, social mixtures, shards, and branching routes.

#### Subchannel A. Filling, Gathering, and Heaping
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Material or people accumulate until a container, place, or heap is full.
- Active motifs: filling abundance `ج م م:B001/m01`; gathering `ل م م:B001/m01`; aggregation `ك ث ر:B007/m01`; gathered pile `ص ب ب:B005/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:20 (جما); 89:19 (لما); 89:12 (أكثروا); 89:13 (صب)
- Synthesis: The motifs describe one scalar process from individual additions to a full mass or visible heap.

#### Subchannel B. Rows and Dense Assemblies
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A multitude is organized into rows at a common place.
- Active motifs: ordered rows `ص ف ف:B001/m01`; crowded assembly `د ك ك:B008/m01`; large group `ر ب ب:B004/m01`; mass of people `ج ن ن:B013/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:22 (صفا صفا); 89:21 (دكا); 89:6, 89:13-16, 89:22, 89:28 (رب); 89:30 (جنتي)
- Synthesis: Density does not remain amorphous: the crowd is sorted into aligned collective presence.

#### Subchannel C. Commingling and the Interloper
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Distinct materials or social elements are mixed, with an inserted outsider becoming part of the compound.
- Active motifs: commingled mixture `ب ل ل:B009/m01`; mixing action `س و ط:B001/m01`; inserted insider or interloper `د خ ل:B005/m01`; likeness within the blend `م ث ل:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:17 (بل); 89:13 (سوط); 89:29-30 (ادخلي); 89:8 (مثلها)
- Synthesis: A physical blend and a social mixture share the same role map: heterogeneous elements, insertion, and a resulting composite.

#### Subchannel D. Fragmentation and Scattering
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A gathered whole breaks into portions that move away in different directions.
- Active motifs: scattering `ب ل و:B009/m01`; dispersal `ب ل ي:B008/m01`; branching separation `ع ب د:B010/m01`; dissipating division `ص ب ب:B007/m01`; shards or portions `ع ش ر:B009/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:15-16 (ابتلاه); 89:29 (عبادي); 89:13 (صب); 89:2 (عشر)
- Synthesis: The scene proceeds from fracture to directional spread; the shard sense supplies the concrete unit produced by division.

### 3. Shaped Ground and Built Supports
- Semantic invariant: Stable places are made by cutting, leveling, bounding, supporting, and directing material.
- Surface relation: direct; 89:7-10 and 89:21-22 foreground columns, carved rock, valley, pegs, leveled earth, and rows.
- Surprising reach: Monumental construction extends into dams, reservoirs, polished surfaces, bodily marks, and load-bearing joints.

#### Subchannel A. Cutting Rock Into a Valley Place
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: Hard stone is pierced or excavated within a valley to create usable space.
- Active motifs: piercing or excavation `ج و ب:B001/m01`; hard stone `ح ج ر:B003/m01`; boulder `ص خ ر:B001/m01`; valley `و د ي:B005/m01`; water-holding rock hollow `خ ل ق:B011/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:9 (جابوا، الصخر، الواد); 89:5 (حجر); 89:8 (يخلق)
- Synthesis: Tool-like cutting acts on resistant material in a valley setting, producing a hollow that can become chamber or reservoir.

#### Subchannel B. Crushing and Leveling the Ground
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Raised or resistant terrain is pounded down into a low, even surface.
- Active motifs: crushing and leveling `د ك ك:B001/m01`; flat low ground `د ك ك:B002/m01`; earth `ء ر ض:B001/m01`; adhesion to ground `ب ل د:B010/m01`; bare level surface `ص ف ف:B005/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:21 (دكت الأرض دكا دكا); 89:8, 89:11 (البلاد); 89:22 (صفا)
- Synthesis: Repeated impact changes relief into a stable plane. The adhesion motif marks the outcome as contact with, rather than elevation above, the ground.

#### Subchannel C. Columns, Pegs, and Reinforcement
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Upright members are fixed and interlocked to carry or stabilize a structure.
- Active motifs: column `ع م د:B003/m01`; peg `و ت د:B001/m01`; supporting shaft `س ر ي:B005/m01`; structural cohesion `م ل ك:B001/m01`; reinforcement `و ث ق:B002/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:7 (العماد); 89:10 (الأوتاد); 89:4 (يسر); 89:22 (الملك); 89:26 (وثاقه)
- Synthesis: The scene combines vertical support, anchoring, and cohesion. Binding is the mechanism that turns separate members into a durable frame.

#### Subchannel D. Precinct, Chamber, and Protected Interior
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A boundary encloses an interior and regulates who or what may enter.
- Active motifs: enclosure or chamber `ح ج ر:B004/m01`; town or bounded land `ب ل د:B001/m01`; concealed garden `ج ن ن:B003/m01`; hiding place `ج ن ن:B017/m01`; restriction at the boundary `ح ج ر:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:5 (حجر); 89:8, 89:11 (البلاد); 89:30 (جنتي)
- Synthesis: Boundary, interior, and controlled access make a single precinct scene, with the garden as a protected cultivated interior.

#### Subchannel E. Channel, Dam, and Reservoir
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A valley channel is constricted by a dam so water gathers in a basin.
- Active motifs: descending channel `ص ب ب:B002/m01`; valley center `م ل ك:B006/m01`; valley `و د ي:B005/m01`; dam `ع م د:B013/m01`; basin `ج ي ء:B002/m01`; rock reservoir `خ ل ق:B011/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:13 (صب); 89:22 (الملك، جاء); 89:9 (الواد); 89:7 (العماد); 89:8 (يخلق)
- Synthesis: Slope conducts water, the dam interrupts it, and the hollow receives it. This is a complete hydraulic mechanism rather than a loose water cluster.

#### Subchannel F. Smoothing, Paving, and Finishing
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A rough surface is worked until it is even, traversable, or polished.
- Active motifs: smooth formed surface `خ ل ق:B008/m01`; level finish `ص ف ف:B005/m01`; paved or treated way `ع ب د:B005/m01`; persistent finishing motion `ص ب ب:B010/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:8 (يخلق); 89:22 (صفا); 89:29 (عبادي); 89:13 (صب)
- Synthesis: Formation continues beyond gross construction into finishing: repeated work removes irregularity and makes a surface usable.

### 4. Water Moving Into Life
- Semantic invariant: Water changes location and form while enabling fertility, nourishment, and habitation.
- Surface relation: indirect; 89:1, 89:9, 89:13, and 89:16-18 anchor bursting, valley, pouring, provision, and food.
- Surprising reach: The hydrologic sequence extends into bubbles, pasture, livestock schedules, fruit ripening, sap, and medicinal extracts.

#### Subchannel A. Rain Returning Life to Soil
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Rain arrives, revives soil, and establishes fresh pasture.
- Active motifs: life-giving rain `ح ي ي:B002/m01`; rain as provision `ر ز ق:B003/m01`; returning water `ر ج ع:B006/m01`; first rain and pasture `ر ص د:B004/m01`; good rain and fertile soil `ك ر م:B002/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:24 (حياتي); 89:16 (رزقه); 89:28 (ارجعي); 89:14 (المرصاد); 89:15, 89:17 (أكرم)
- Synthesis: Arrival, return, revived ground, and pasture form a causal ecological sequence from weather to renewed life.

#### Subchannel B. Spring, Pour, and Surface Bubbles
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Water breaks out, pours forward, fills, and visibly bubbles at the surface.
- Active motifs: water-burst `ف ج ر:B001/m01`; pouring `ص ب ب:B001/m01`; water bubbles `ح ب ب:B008/m01`; filling water `ج م م:B001/m01`; opening or release `ن ف س:B009/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:1 (الفجر); 89:13 (صب); 89:20 (حبا جما); 89:27 (النفس)
- Synthesis: The same rupture that names dawn also yields a concrete spring scene: release, flow, accumulation, and surface agitation.

#### Subchannel C. Torrent and Overflow
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Accumulating water exceeds its bounds and becomes an overwhelming flood.
- Active motifs: flood excess `ط غ و:B002/m01`; flood surge `ط غ ي:B002/m01`; abundant gathered water `ر ب ب:B013/m01`; fullness `ج م م:B001/m01`; poured mass `ص ب ب:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:11 (طغوا); 89:6, 89:13-16, 89:22, 89:28 (رب); 89:20 (جما); 89:13 (صب)
- Synthesis: Quantity becomes force when containment fails. This hydraulic transformation provides a concrete counterpart to moral transgression.

#### Subchannel D. Sweet Water, Drinking, and Watering Cycles
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Sweet water is drawn, drunk, and returned to livestock on a regular watering schedule.
- Active motifs: sweet potable water `ع ذ ب:B001/m01`; drinking or filling `ح ب ب:B006/m01`; leading animals to drink again `د خ ل:B007/m01`; watering interval `ع ش ر:B006/m01`; draught `ن ف س:B006/m01`; survival water `م ل ك:B007/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:13, 89:25 (عذاب); 89:20 (حبا); 89:29-30 (ادخلي); 89:2 (عشر); 89:27 (النفس); 89:22 (الملك)
- Synthesis: The scene has source, consumer, herd movement, and recurrence. Water is both drink and the resource that makes stopping in a place possible.

#### Subchannel E. Vegetation and Garden Growth
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Moist fertile ground produces dense vegetation and a cultivated garden.
- Active motifs: fertile soil `ء ر ض:B002/m01`; dense vegetation `ج ن ن:B011/m01`; cultivated plant `ر ب ب:B012/m01`; gathered growth `ج م م:B005/m01`; plant among rocks `ص خ ر:B003/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:21 (الأرض); 89:30 (جنتي); 89:6, 89:13-16, 89:22, 89:28 (رب); 89:20 (جما); 89:9 (الصخر)
- Synthesis: Soil, plant, density, and bounded garden form one growth scene, including vegetation that occupies otherwise hard terrain.

#### Subchannel F. Seed, Fruit, and Ripening
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Seed becomes crop, fruit matures, and a vine or palm yields edible produce.
- Active motifs: seed `ح ب ب:B001/m01`; crop yield `ء ك ل:B002/m01`; ripening `ط ع م:B005/m01`; vine or grape `ك ر م:B004/m01`; palm pith `ك ث ر:B006/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:20 (حبا); 89:19 (تأكلون، أكلا); 89:18 (طعام); 89:15, 89:17 (أكرم); 89:12 (أكثروا)
- Synthesis: The motifs trace a complete agricultural transformation from kernel through ripeness to harvestable fruit.

#### Subchannel G. Sap, Extract, and Medicinal Paste
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Plant material is pressed into juice or resin and thickened into a bitter medicinal preparation.
- Active motifs: bitter drug or gum `ح ض ض:B003/m01`; plant extract `ص ب ب:B006/m01`; thick syrup `ر ب ب:B006/m01`; medicinal dressing `ر ب ب:B006/m02`
- Ayah anchors: 89:18 (تحاضون); 89:13 (صب، ربك); 89:6, 89:14-16, 89:22, 89:28 (رب)
- Synthesis: Extraction and thickening connect vegetation to treatment: liquid sap becomes a prepared paste applied as medicine.

### 5. Food, Preparation, and Containment
- Semantic invariant: Edible material becomes usable through tasting, cutting, cooking, and containment.
- Surface relation: direct; 89:18-19 foreground feeding, food, and eating.
- Surprising reach: The food scene expands into roasting strips, date stew, lidded jars, hunting tools, and ascetic abstention.

#### Subchannel A. Eating, Taste, and Palatability
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Food is apportioned, tasted, and consumed according to its sweetness or suitability.
- Active motifs: eating `ء ك ل:B001/m01`; tasting or eating `ط ع م:B001/m01`; food provision `ر ز ق:B002/m01`; sweet food or water `ع ذ ب:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:19 (تأكلون، أكلا); 89:18 (طعام); 89:16 (رزقه); 89:13, 89:25 (عذاب)
- Synthesis: Consumption is grounded in an edible object, sensory evaluation, and allotted provision rather than in appetite alone.

#### Subchannel B. Roasting, Pot, and Stew
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Meat or plant food is cut into strips, roasted, and cooked in a pot or liquid stew.
- Active motifs: roasted strips `ص ف ف:B003/m01`; cooking pot `ق د ر:B007/m01`; vessel `ء ك ل:B013/m01`; date stew `س و ط:B004/m01`; thick cooking base `ر ب ب:B006/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:22 (صفا); 89:16 (قدر); 89:19 (أكل); 89:13 (سوط، ربك)
- Synthesis: A vessel receives prepared strips and liquid ingredients, yielding a single culinary process from cutting to cooked mixture.

#### Subchannel C. Jar, Bowl, Basket, and Lid
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A rigid or woven container receives material and is closed or covered.
- Active motifs: jar `ح ب ب:B007/m01`; vessel or bowl `ء ن ي:B004/m01`; pottery vessel `ص خ ر:B002/m01`; palm basket `د خ ل:B010/m01`; lid `ك ر م:B005/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:20 (حبا); 89:23 (أنى); 89:9 (الصخر); 89:29-30 (ادخلي); 89:15, 89:17 (أكرم)
- Synthesis: Container body, material, woven alternative, and lid form a compact artifact scene centered on storage.

#### Subchannel D. Hunting, Carcass, and Edible Meat
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: An animal is hunted or selected, fattened, cut, and made edible.
- Active motifs: hunting implement `ط ع م:B006/m01`; prey or fattened animal `ء ك ل:B007/m01`; breeding animal `ح ج ر:B007/m01`; animal fatness `ط ع م:B007/m01`; meat or haunch `ء ل ي:B015/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:18 (طعام); 89:19 (أكل); 89:5 (حجر); 89:28 (إلى)
- Synthesis: Tool, animal, body portion, and edible outcome make this a hunting-and-butchery scene distinct from cooking in a vessel.

#### Subchannel E. Abstention and the Withheld Mouthful
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Eating or drinking is deliberately prevented, producing deprivation or disciplined abstention.
- Active motifs: abstaining from food or drink `ع ذ ب:B002/m01`; prevention or weaning `ع ذ ب:B003/m01`; throat grip `ط ع م:B012/m01`; restraint `ح ج ر:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:13, 89:25 (عذاب); 89:18 (طعام); 89:5 (حجر)
- Synthesis: The scene reverses nourishment: access is blocked at the boundary of consumption, either as discipline, weaning, or coercive deprivation.

### 6. Animal Bodies, Herds, and Habitats
- Semantic invariant: Animal life is organized through habitat, herd movement, breeding, and distinctive body parts.
- Surface relation: indirect; 89:2, 89:8-9, 89:15, and 89:18 provide counting, land, valley, favor, and feeding anchors.
- Surprising reach: The network reaches ostrich nests, returning birds, milk pails, serpents, a small wild ox, and termite damage.

#### Subchannel A. Herd, Pasture, and Subsistence
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Livestock gather as a herd, remain near pasture, and supply subsistence.
- Active motifs: herd `ر ب ب:B014/m01`; livestock `ن ع م:B005/m01`; gathered herd `ص ب ب:B005/m02`; animal fatness `ط ع م:B007/m01`; sustaining pasture `س ك ن:B010/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:6, 89:13-16, 89:22, 89:28 (رب); 89:15 (نعمه); 89:13 (صب); 89:18 (طعام، المسكين)
- Synthesis: Grouped animals, grazing place, and bodily condition form a pastoral subsistence scene.

#### Subchannel B. Milking, Udder, and Pail
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A milk-bearing animal is secured, milkings are combined, and yield is collected in a vessel.
- Active motifs: milk-filled animal `ص ف ف:B002/m01`; combined milkings `ش ف ع:B005/m01`; udder braces `و د ي:B006/m01`; pregnancy or lactation period `ع ش ر:B007/m01`; collection vessel `ر ب ب:B010/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:22 (صفا); 89:3 (الشفع); 89:9 (الواد); 89:2 (عشر); 89:6, 89:13-16, 89:22, 89:28 (رب)
- Synthesis: Animal, udder support, repeated milking, and pail produce a complete dairy-work scene.

#### Subchannel C. Ostrich Nest, Egg, and Primary Feathers
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: An ostrich occupies a ground nest, leaves an egg, and is recognized by its forward primary feathers and shape.
- Active motifs: ostrich nest and egg `ب ل د:B012/m01`; ostrich `ن ع م:B006/m01`; ostrich-like form `ن ع م:B007/m01`; primary feathers `ع ش ر:B017/m01`; bird forepart `ق د م:B007/m02`
- Ayah anchors: 89:8, 89:11 (البلاد); 89:15 (نعمه); 89:2 (عشر); 89:24 (قدمت)
- Synthesis: Habitat, egg, species, shape, and feather anatomy cohere as one concrete desert-bird scene.

#### Subchannel D. Bird in Thicket, Cave, and Return
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A bird shelters in a cave or dense tree cover, leaves, and returns by a familiar route.
- Active motifs: bird habitat in cave or tree `د خ ل:B009/m01`; dense cover `ج ن ن:B011/m01`; returning birds `ر ج ع:B012/m01`; nightingale-like call `ب ل ل:B010/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:29-30 (ادخلي); 89:30 (جنتي); 89:28 (ارجعي); 89:17 (بل)
- Synthesis: Shelter, movement, return, and call define a habitat cycle rather than a generic wildlife grouping.

#### Subchannel E. Serpent and Viper
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A hard, sinuous reptile appears from concealment and is identified as snake or viper.
- Active motifs: serpent `ج ن ن:B012/m01`; snake `ح ي ي:B004/m01`; bodily hardness `ذ ك ر:B002/m01`; concealment `ج ن ن:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:30 (جنتي); 89:24 (حياتي); 89:23 (الذكرى)
- Synthesis: Concealment and sudden reptile presence bind the two independent snake senses into one animal scene.

#### Subchannel F. Small Wild Animal in a Hollow
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A small wild animal occupies a cave-like hollow within herd or scrub terrain.
- Active motifs: small wild ox `ط غ و:B009/m01`; smallness `ح ب ب:B010/m01`; cave or tree habitat `د خ ل:B009/m01`; herd setting `ر ب ب:B014/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:11 (طغوا); 89:20 (حبا); 89:29-30 (ادخلي); 89:6, 89:13-16, 89:22, 89:28 (رب)
- Synthesis: Size, species, habitat, and surrounding herd ecology make a compact wildlife scene.

### 7. Body as Cavity, Surface, and Joint
- Semantic invariant: The body is mapped through enclosing surfaces, openings, projections, and load-bearing joints.
- Surface relation: indirect; 89:5, 89:8, 89:15, 89:20, 89:22, and 89:27 supply the lexical anchors.
- Surprising reach: Architectural and astronomical forms recur as chest, skull, pupil, nostril, hip socket, and sexual anatomy.

#### Subchannel A. Thorax, Lung, Breath, and Nostril
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A chest cavity houses ribs and lung, receives breath, and opens through a divided nostril.
- Active motifs: chest `ب ل د:B002/m01`; broad thorax `ج م م:B009/m01`; ribs or chest bones `ج ن ن:B016/m01`; lung `ر ء ي:B009/m01`; breath `ن ف س:B001/m01`; nostril septum `و ت ر:B009/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:8, 89:11 (البلاد); 89:20 (جما); 89:30 (جنتي); 89:6 (تر); 89:27 (النفس); 89:3 (الوتر)
- Synthesis: Container, supporting frame, organ, moving air, and divided outlet form a complete respiratory anatomy.

#### Subchannel B. Skull, Hair, and Ear Projection
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Hair covers the crown while a small cartilaginous projection marks the side of the head.
- Active motifs: skull crown `ء ر م:B001/m02`; head hair `ج م م:B004/m01`; lock of hair `ل م م:B005/m01`; ear protrusion `و ت د:B002/m01`; central nub or axis `ع م د:B007/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:7 (إرم، العماد); 89:20 (جما); 89:19 (لما); 89:10 (الأوتاد)
- Synthesis: Crown, hair mass, lock, and ear nub compose one head-surface scene linked by elevation and projection.

#### Subchannel C. Face, Pupil, and Double Vision
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A face presents an image to the eye; the pupil receives it, while vision may split into two.
- Active motifs: face `ح ي ي:B012/m01`; facial comeliness `ق س م:B001/m01`; visible appearance `ر ء ي:B006/m01`; pupil or reflected image `ء ن س:B005/m01`; double vision `ش ف ع:B006/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:24 (حياتي); 89:5 (قسم); 89:6 (تر); 89:15, 89:23 (الإنسان); 89:3 (الشفع)
- Synthesis: Surface, reflected image, observer, and visual disturbance form a coherent optics-and-countenance scene.

#### Subchannel D. Mouth, Kiss, Throat, and Breath
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Mouth-to-mouth contact joins two faces while breath and the throat remain the vulnerable passage.
- Active motifs: mouth-to-mouth kiss `ط ع م:B013/m01`; teeth array `ح ب ب:B009/m01`; throat grip `ط ع م:B012/m01`; breath `ن ف س:B001/m01`; fettering pressure `ص ب ب:B009/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:18 (طعام); 89:20 (حبا); 89:27 (النفس); 89:13 (صب)
- Synthesis: The same oral passage supports intimacy and coercion, producing a sharp role reversal between exchanged breath and blocked breath.

#### Subchannel E. Hip Socket, Haunch, and Weight
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A rounded socket receives a limb and bears the weight transmitted through the pelvis or haunch.
- Active motifs: hip socket `ك ر م:B007/m01`; haunch `ء ل ي:B015/m01`; bodily weight in intercourse `د ك ك:B009/m01`; rounded joint-mark `ح ج ر:B006/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:15, 89:17 (أكرم); 89:28 (إلى); 89:21 (دكا); 89:5 (حجر)
- Synthesis: Socket, limb mass, and applied weight form a biomechanical joint scene with a secondary sexual extension.

#### Subchannel F. Erection, Discharge, and Reproductive Passage
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Tumescence precedes discharge into a reproductive passage.
- Active motifs: erection `و ت د:B004/m01`; discharge or erection `و د ي:B001/m01`; bodily hardness `ذ ك ر:B002/m01`; fettered or compressed posture `ص ب ب:B009/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:10 (الأوتاد); 89:9 (الواد); 89:23 (الذكرى); 89:13 (صب)
- Synthesis: Projection, hardness, fluid release, and bodily pressure form a specific sexual-physiology scene.

### 8. Generation, Kinship, and Care
- Semantic invariant: Life is carried forward through gestation, birth, marriage, guardianship, and care for dependents.
- Surface relation: indirect; 89:15-18, 89:24, and 89:27-30 anchor human life, trial, orphanhood, feeding, soul, servants, and garden.
- Surprising reach: The social frame reaches fetal concealment, afterbirth, failed pregnancy, marital return, fosterage, and lap protection.

#### Subchannel A. Living Creature, Soul, and Breath
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A living body is animated by breath and understood as a soul or enduring self.
- Active motifs: living creature `ح ي ي:B003/m01`; soul or life `ن ف س:B011/m01`; breath `ن ف س:B001/m01`; preservation of life `ح ي ي:B006/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:24 (حياتي); 89:27 (النفس)
- Synthesis: Physiology and personhood meet in a single animation scene: breath sustains the creature while soul names its living identity.

#### Subchannel B. Womb, Fetus, Birth, and Afterbirth
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A fetus remains concealed in the womb, emerges in childbirth, and is followed by afterbirth.
- Active motifs: fetus `ج ن ن:B007/m01`; womb or reproductive organ `ح ي ي:B011/m01`; childbirth `ن ف س:B005/m01`; womb and afterbirth `ع ذ ب:B009/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:30 (جنتي); 89:24 (حياتي); 89:27 (النفس); 89:13, 89:25 (عذاب)
- Synthesis: Concealment, maternal organ, emergence, and postpartum material form a complete birth process.

#### Subchannel C. Pregnancy, Gestation, and Failed Return
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Pregnancy becomes visible, advances through a long term, and may end in live birth or failed gestation.
- Active motifs: visible pregnancy `ر ء ي:B010/m01`; pregnant animal `ش ف ع:B004/m01`; ten-month gestation `ع ش ر:B007/m01`; recent birth or ewe `ر ب ب:B009/m01`; failed pregnancy `ر ج ع:B013/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:6 (تر); 89:3 (الشفع); 89:2 (عشر); 89:6, 89:13-16, 89:22, 89:28 (رب); 89:28 (ارجعي)
- Synthesis: Visibility, duration, maternal animal, and outcome distinguish gestation from the separate scene of birth itself.

#### Subchannel D. Marriage, Consummation, and Marital Return
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A marriage contract permits entry and consummation, while separation may be reversed by marital return.
- Active motifs: consummation `د خ ل:B002/m01`; marriage contract `م ل ك:B004/m01`; marital return `ر ج ع:B004/m01`; woman without spouse `ي ت م:B005/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:29-30 (ادخلي); 89:22 (الملك); 89:28 (ارجعي); 89:17 (اليتيم)
- Synthesis: Contract, sexual entry, separation status, and return form one legal and domestic marriage frame.

#### Subchannel E. Fosterage, Orphanhood, and Lap Protection
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A guardian receives a dependent child, protects it close to the body, and supplies care.
- Active motifs: foster child or guardian `ر ب ب:B005/m01`; orphan `ي ت م:B001/m01`; lap or bosom `ح ج ر:B005/m01`; urging care `ح ض ض:B001/m01`; nurture and repair `ر ب ب:B002/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:6, 89:13-16, 89:22, 89:28 (رب); 89:17 (اليتيم); 89:5 (حجر); 89:18 (تحاضون)
- Synthesis: Guardian, dependent, protective bodily space, and prompted care make the orphan command part of a wider fosterage scene.

### 9. Provision, Property, and Redistribution
- Semantic invariant: Goods acquire moral and social meaning through allotment, withholding, transfer, and use.
- Surface relation: direct; 89:15-20 contrasts provision and restriction with orphan care, feeding, inheritance, and love of wealth.
- Surprising reach: The same economy includes pay, tithe, revenue, gifts for return, preemption, and compensation for loss.

#### Subchannel A. Trial by Abundance and Restriction
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: A person receives either honor and ease or restricted provision and interprets the allotment as status.
- Active motifs: testing and disclosure `ب ل و:B002/m01`; measured restriction `ق د ر:B004/m01`; allotted provision `ر ز ق:B001/m01`; blessing `ن ع م:B001/m01`; honor `ك ر م:B001/m01`; humiliation `ه و ن:B003/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:15-16 (ابتلاه، قدر، رزقه، نعمه، أكرمه، أهانن)
- Synthesis: The scene directly stages two allocations as a test and exposes the error of equating resource level with personal worth.

#### Subchannel B. Feeding the Vulnerable
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: Community members urge one another to feed a poor or dependent person.
- Active motifs: mutual urging `ح ض ض:B001/m01`; feeding `ط ع م:B002/m01`; poverty and humility `س ك ن:B006/m01`; orphanhood `ي ت م:B001/m01`; generous honor `ك ر م:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:17-18 (تكرمون اليتيم، تحاضون، طعام المسكين)
- Synthesis: Need, food, exhortation, and honor form a welfare scene in which dignity is enacted through provision.

#### Subchannel C. Devouring Inheritance and Hoarding Wealth
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: Inherited property is seized wholesale and added to an already loved mass of wealth.
- Active motifs: consuming or seizing wealth `ء ك ل:B004/m01`; inheritance `و ر ث:B001/m01`; wealth `م و ل:B001/m01`; intense love `ح ب ب:B002/m01`; abundance `ج م م:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:19-20 (تأكلون التراث أكلا لما، تحبون المال حبا جما)
- Synthesis: Transfer becomes predation when the recipient treats inherited property as consumable mass rather than an obligation.

#### Subchannel D. Ration, Pay, Tithe, and Share
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A common resource is measured into due shares, including ration, wages, and a tenth levy.
- Active motifs: ration or portion `ء ك ل:B003/m01`; soldiers' pay `ر ز ق:B004/m01`; division into shares `ق س م:B003/m01`; collecting a tithe `ع ش ر:B004/m01`; measured limit `ق د ر:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:19 (أكل); 89:16 (رزقه، قدر); 89:5 (قسم); 89:2 (عشر)
- Synthesis: Measure, division, institutional pay, and tithe form a concrete allocation system.

#### Subchannel E. Gift, Hospitality, and Reciprocal Reward
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A benefit is offered to a guest or beneficiary with blessing, return, or recompense.
- Active motifs: benefit or gift `ب ل ل:B002/m01`; returning benefit `ع و د:B006/m01`; gift seeking return `ك ر م:B008/m01`; blessing `ن ع م:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:17 (بل); 89:6 (عاد); 89:15, 89:17 (أكرم); 89:15 (نعمه)
- Synthesis: Transfer is reciprocal but not predatory: gift, reception, blessing, and return create a hospitality economy.

#### Subchannel F. Income, Revenue, and Receipt
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Goods enter a household or estate as income and may pass onward as an uncontracted grant.
- Active motifs: income `د خ ل:B006/m01`; recurring benefit `ع و د:B006/m01`; grant without contract `و ر ث:B002/m01`; livelihood `ط ع م:B004/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:29-30 (ادخلي); 89:6 (عاد); 89:19 (التراث); 89:18 (طعام)
- Synthesis: The literal notion of entry is economicized: receipt becomes revenue, livelihood, and transferable benefit.

#### Subchannel G. Preemption and Annexed Property
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: An owner claims priority over an adjacent share and incorporates it into existing property.
- Active motifs: preemption or annexed share `ش ف ع:B003/m01`; ownership `م ل ك:B002/m01`; lordship or possession `ر ب ب:B001/m01`; divided share `ق س م:B003/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:3 (الشفع); 89:22 (الملك); 89:6, 89:13-16, 89:22, 89:28 (رب); 89:5 (قسم)
- Synthesis: Pairing becomes a legal mechanism: adjacency creates priority, and the claimed share joins a larger holding.

### 10. Character and Public Standing
- Semantic invariant: Inner disposition becomes socially legible through conduct, generosity, reputation, imitation, or corruption.
- Surface relation: direct; 89:11-20 contrasts corruption and acquisitiveness with honor, generosity, and care.
- Surprising reach: Character is carried by face, rumor, habitual pace, counterfeit action, and rivalry in giving.

#### Subchannel A. Generous Character in Action
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A noble disposition issues in generous deeds and abundant giving.
- Active motifs: generosity and honor `ك ر م:B001/m01`; noble act `ف ع ل:B002/m01`; overflowing generosity `ف ج ر:B005/m01`; noble character `ع ذ ب:B008/m01`; good disposition `خ ل ق:B004/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:15, 89:17 (أكرم); 89:6 (فعل); 89:1 (الفجر); 89:13, 89:25 (عذاب); 89:8 (يخلق)
- Synthesis: Disposition, deed, and abundant benefit align as one virtue scene; generosity is an action pattern, not a possession.

#### Subchannel B. Companionship and Social Character
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Character is tested and displayed in sustained social mixing and companionship.
- Active motifs: social companionship `ع ش ر:B012/m01`; character and vigor `ن ف س:B014/m01`; familiarity `ء ن س:B003/m01`; comfort in company `س ك ن:B004/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:2 (عشر); 89:27 (النفس); 89:15, 89:23 (الإنسان); 89:18 (المسكين)
- Synthesis: Repeated association supplies the setting in which disposition becomes observable as ease, strength, or strain.

#### Subchannel C. Honor, Reputation, and Approval
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Praiseworthy action produces mention, reputation, and an approving response.
- Active motifs: reputation `ذ ك ر:B007/m01`; praise or desire `ح ب ب:B003/m01`; approving response `ك ر م:B009/m01`; commendation `ن ع م:B003/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:23 (الذكرى); 89:20 (حبا); 89:15, 89:17 (أكرم); 89:15 (نعمه)
- Synthesis: Conduct circulates as public mention and returns to the actor as praise or approval.

#### Subchannel D. Ostentation, Gossip, and Inflated Speech
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A person performs for visibility while rumor and excessive speech amplify the performance.
- Active motifs: ostentation `ر ء ي:B005/m01`; gossip or rumor `ء ك ل:B009/m01`; circulated report `ق و ل:B007/m01`; wealth or verbosity `ك ث ر:B003/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:6 (تر); 89:19 (أكل); 89:15-16, 89:24 (يقول); 89:12 (أكثروا)
- Synthesis: Display supplies the act, rumor supplies transmission, and verbal excess enlarges reputation beyond the deed.

#### Subchannel E. Hidden Defect, Corruption, and Fabrication
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A concealed flaw is covered by fabricated action or speech until corruption spreads.
- Active motifs: hidden defect or deceit `د خ ل:B004/m01`; corruption `ف س د:B001/m01`; fabricated lie `خ ل ق:B007/m01`; contrived act `ف ع ل:B004/m01`; breach or immorality `ف ج ر:B004/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:29-30 (ادخلي); 89:12 (الفساد); 89:8 (يخلق); 89:6 (فعل); 89:1 (الفجر)
- Synthesis: The scene moves from inward flaw to outward fabrication and finally to social damage.

#### Subchannel F. Habit, Method, and Imitation
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Repeated action hardens into a method that others may imitate.
- Active motifs: habit `ع و د:B004/m01`; regular method `و ت ر:B005/m01`; imitation `م ث ل:B010/m01`; nurture or training `ر ب ب:B002/m01`; steady pace `ص ب ب:B010/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:6 (عاد); 89:3 (الوتر); 89:8 (مثلها); 89:6, 89:13-16, 89:22, 89:28 (رب); 89:13 (صب)
- Synthesis: Recurrence transforms an act into a norm; imitation then carries that norm into another agent's conduct.

### 11. Perception, Thought, and Memory
- Semantic invariant: Information moves from sensory detection through inner judgment into retained guidance.
- Surface relation: direct; 89:5-6, 89:14, and 89:23-24 foreground intellect, seeing, watching, remembering, and regretful speech.
- Surprising reach: The channel includes diplopia, the evil eye, divided planning, inner speech, madness, and legal memory.

#### Subchannel A. Seeing, Detecting, and Showing
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: An observer detects a visible sign while a watcher or presenter brings it into view.
- Active motifs: seeing `ر ء ي:B001/m01`; perception `ء ن س:B002/m01`; showing `ر ء ي:B012/m01`; watch `ر ص د:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:6 (تر); 89:15, 89:23 (الإنسان); 89:14 (المرصاد)
- Synthesis: Observer, displayed object, and vigilant station form a complete detection scene.

#### Subchannel B. Pupil, Double Vision, and Harmful Gaze
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: The pupil receives an image, but vision may split or become a vehicle of harm.
- Active motifs: pupil or reflected image `ء ن س:B005/m01`; double vision `ش ف ع:B006/m01`; evil eye `ل م م:B007/m01`; harmful gaze `ن ف س:B003/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:15, 89:23 (الإنسان); 89:3 (الشفع); 89:19 (لما); 89:27 (النفس)
- Synthesis: The visual apparatus and its failure modes share one role map: image, eye, distorted perception, and injury.

#### Subchannel C. Deliberate Measure and Planning
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A projected act is measured, divided into alternatives, and selected through deliberation.
- Active motifs: conceptual measuring `خ ل ق:B001/m01`; planning `ق د ر:B005/m01`; considered opinion `ر ء ي:B002/m01`; divided deliberation `ق س م:B006/m01`; heart or inner center `ج ن ن:B010/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:8 (يخلق); 89:16 (قدر); 89:6 (تر); 89:5 (قسم); 89:30 (جنتي)
- Synthesis: The scene contains object, alternatives, internal arena, and decision procedure; measure is cognitive before it becomes material.

#### Subchannel D. Inner Speech and Reason
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: The self formulates unspoken propositions and evaluates them by intellect or prudence.
- Active motifs: inner speech `ق و ل:B012/m01`; mind or self `ن ف س:B013/m01`; intellect `ح ج ر:B002/m01`; judgment or value `ط ع م:B008/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:15-16, 89:24 (يقول); 89:27 (النفس); 89:5 (حجر); 89:18 (طعام)
- Synthesis: Thought is modeled as speech held inside the self and constrained by an evaluative faculty.

#### Subchannel E. Madness and Loss of Prudence
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Intellect is displaced by madness or an afflicting touch.
- Active motifs: madness `ج ن ن:B006/m01`; derangement attributed to earth spirits `ء ر ض:B012/m01`; jinn touch `ل م م:B004/m01`; intellect `ح ج ر:B002/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:30 (جنتي); 89:21 (الأرض); 89:19 (لما); 89:5 (حجر)
- Synthesis: The scene is a contrastive role reversal: the faculty that restrains thought is overcome by an externalized affliction.

#### Subchannel F. Remembrance, Warning, and Learned Sign
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A prior event is recalled and converted into a warning or teachable sign.
- Active motifs: remembering `ذ ك ر:B003/m01`; reminder `ذ ك ر:B009/m01`; sign or lesson `م ث ل:B011/m01`; inherited knowledge `و ر ث:B003/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:23 (يتذكر، الذكرى); 89:8 (مثلها); 89:19 (التراث)
- Synthesis: Memory becomes guidance when a retained event is framed as a sign and transmitted as knowledge.

### 12. Speech, Logic, and Reference
- Semantic invariant: Language connects speaker, proposition, answer, and referent through distinct discourse operations.
- Surface relation: direct; 89:5-6, 89:15-16, and 89:23-24 contain questions, assertions, and reported speech.
- Surprising reach: The discourse system extends into corrective particles, pronouns, definitions, rumor, and phonetic articulation.

#### Subchannel A. Question, How, and Whence
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A speaker seeks identity, manner, source, or possibility through an interrogative.
- Active motifs: interrogative whence or how `ء ن ي:B005/m01`; “tell me” inquiry `ر ء ي:B013/m01`; interrogative “what” `ذ و و:B004/m01`; supposition in a question `ق و ل:B011/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:23 (أنى); 89:6 (تر كيف); 89:5, 89:10 (ذي); 89:15-16, 89:24 (يقول)
- Synthesis: These motifs specify complementary question roles: unknown source, requested perception, queried entity, and proposed answer-space.

#### Subchannel B. Answer, Reply, and Affirmation
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A question receives a reply that may correct a negation or affirm a proposition.
- Active motifs: answer `ج و ب:B003/m01`; reply `ر ج ع:B005/m01`; affirmative answer `ب ل ي:B010/m01`; corrective “yes, indeed” `ب ل و:B006/m01`; “yes” `ن ع م:B004/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:9 (جابوا); 89:28 (ارجعي); 89:15-16 (ابتلاه، نعمه)
- Synthesis: Answer, return, correction, and affirmation form a dialogue sequence with explicit polarity control.

#### Subchannel C. Utterance, Tongue, and Articulation
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: An intended proposition is shaped by the tongue into audible speech.
- Active motifs: utterance `ق و ل:B001/m01`; tongue or articulator `ق و ل:B002/m01`; clear articulation `ب ل ل:B006/m01`; unclear speech `ج م م:B008/m01`; mention `ذ ك ر:B004/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:15-16, 89:24 (يقول); 89:17 (بل); 89:20 (جما); 89:23 (الذكرى)
- Synthesis: Content, organ, articulation quality, and social mention form one speech-production scene.

#### Subchannel D. Rumor and Circulating Report
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A report passes among people, gathering distortion and social force as it circulates.
- Active motifs: rumor `ق و ل:B007/m01`; gossip `ء ك ل:B009/m01`; unclear or crowded speech `ج م م:B008/m01`; dispersal among routes `ع ب د:B010/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:15-16, 89:24 (يقول); 89:19 (أكل); 89:20 (جما); 89:29 (عبادي)
- Synthesis: A single utterance becomes socially distributed speech, with crowding and branching transmission accounting for distortion.

#### Subchannel E. Definition, Description, and Essence
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A term is bounded by a definition that describes the essential identity of its object.
- Active motifs: definition `ق و ل:B016/m01`; description `م ث ل:B004/m01`; essence `ن ف س:B012/m01`; limit or measure `ق د ر:B001/m01`; signification `ق و ل:B014/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:15-16, 89:24 (يقول); 89:8 (مثلها); 89:27 (النفس); 89:16 (قدر)
- Synthesis: Sign, boundary, description, and essence form a coherent semantic-definition operation.

#### Subchannel F. Particle, Pronoun, and Complement
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Grammatical particles and referential forms connect a clause to its participant or complement.
- Active motifs: corrective particle `ب ل ل:B008/m01`; relative pronoun `ذ و و:B002/m01`; locative or relational preposition `ء ل ي:B002/m01`; grammatical object `ف ع ل:B007/m01`; quantifying particle `ر ب ب:B015/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:17 (بل); 89:5, 89:10 (ذي); 89:28 (إلى); 89:6 (فعل); 89:6, 89:13-16, 89:22, 89:28 (رب)
- Synthesis: The motifs form clause architecture: correction reopens the proposition, pronoun identifies a referent, and preposition or object assigns relation.

### 13. Covenant, Restraint, and Reparation
- Semantic invariant: A relation becomes durable when it is sworn, trusted, physically secured, or repaired after breach.
- Surface relation: direct; 89:5 gives oath, while 89:26 gives binding.
- Surprising reach: Binding extends from testimony and truce to marriage, surety, fetter, and blood compensation.

#### Subchannel A. Oath, Testimony, and Pledge
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A speaker makes a claim binding by oath and confirms it through testimony.
- Active motifs: oath `ء ل ي:B007/m01`; oath or pledge `ق س م:B004/m01`; sworn affirmation `ب ل ي:B005/m01`; covenant `و ث ق:B004/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:28 (إلى); 89:5 (قسم); 89:15-16 (ابتلاه); 89:26 (وثاقه)
- Synthesis: Utterance, solemn act, affirmation, and durable obligation form one pledge scene.

#### Subchannel B. Household Pact and Truce
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A household or encampment enters a pact that suspends conflict and stabilizes residence.
- Active motifs: household `س ك ن:B003/m01`; tent community `ع م د:B004/m01`; covenant `ر ب ب:B011/m01`; truce `ق س م:B008/m01`; firm pact `و ث ق:B004/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:18 (المسكين); 89:7 (العماد); 89:6, 89:13-16, 89:22, 89:28 (رب); 89:5 (قسم); 89:26 (وثاقه)
- Synthesis: Participants, shared dwelling, cessation of conflict, and binding assurance produce a social-contract scene.

#### Subchannel C. Fetter, Shackle, and Fixed Body
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A person is placed in a restraint and fixed so movement is prevented.
- Active motifs: binding `و ث ق:B003/m01`; fettering `ص ب ب:B009/m01`; restraint or prohibition `ح ج ر:B001/m01`; prevention `ع ذ ب:B003/m01`; upright fixation `و ت د:B003/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:26 (يوثق، وثاقه); 89:13 (صب); 89:5 (حجر); 89:13, 89:25 (عذاب); 89:10 (الأوتاد)
- Synthesis: Device, action, restricted participant, and immobile outcome make a concrete coercive mechanism.

#### Subchannel D. Trust, Surety, and Guarantor
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A guarantor secures another party's reliance through trust and accepted obligation.
- Active motifs: trust `و ث ق:B001/m01`; obedient guarantor `ر ض و:B006/m01`; firm need or knot `ر ب ب:B016/m01`; acceptance `ر ض ي:B004/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:26 (وثاقه); 89:28 (راضية مرضية); 89:6, 89:13-16, 89:22, 89:28 (رب)
- Synthesis: Reliance rests on a person who accepts and secures an obligation, converting affection or approval into surety.

#### Subchannel E. Bloodmoney and Reparation
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Loss through death or injury creates a claim answered by compensatory payment.
- Active motifs: bloodmoney `و د ي:B002/m01`; bereavement or vendetta `و ت ر:B002/m01`; divided compensation `ق س م:B003/m01`; prepared recompense `ك ر م:B008/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:9 (الواد); 89:3 (الوتر); 89:5 (قسم); 89:15, 89:17 (أكرم)
- Synthesis: Injury, aggrieved claimant, measured payment, and settlement form a legal reparation scene.

#### Subchannel F. Marriage as Binding Contract
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A marriage relation is secured by contract and may be maintained or restored through accepted obligation.
- Active motifs: marriage contract `م ل ك:B004/m01`; marital return `ر ج ع:B004/m01`; covenant `و ث ق:B004/m01`; consummation `د خ ل:B002/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:22 (الملك); 89:28 (ارجعي); 89:26 (وثاقه); 89:29-30 (ادخلي)
- Synthesis: Contract, entry, continuity, and return give marriage the same durable relational structure as other covenants.

### 14. Rule, Transgression, and Judgment
- Semantic invariant: Power establishes order, exceeds its bounds, watches for response, and imposes sanction.
- Surface relation: direct; 89:6-14 and 89:25-26 present lords, rulers, transgression, corruption, punishment, surveillance, and unique binding.
- Surprising reach: Political authority resonates with ownership, bodily mastery, idol allegiance, exemplary punishment, and prepared traps.

#### Subchannel A. Ownership, Dominion, and Mastery
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A possessor exercises control over property, people, or a bounded domain.
- Active motifs: lord or owner `ر ب ب:B001/m01`; ownership `م ل ك:B002/m01`; dominion `م ل ك:B003/m01`; power or mastery `ق د ر:B003/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:6, 89:13-16, 89:22, 89:28 (رب); 89:22 (الملك); 89:16 (قدر)
- Synthesis: Possessor, holding, domain, and capacity form the basic structure from which governance and coercion diverge.

#### Subchannel B. Chief, Ruler, and Hierarchy
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A titled leader stands at the center of a group and serves as its main support.
- Active motifs: ruler title `ق و ل:B004/m01`; chief `ع م د:B006/m01`; captain or leader `ر ب ب:B017/m01`; mainstay `م ل ك:B005/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:15-16, 89:24 (يقول); 89:7 (العماد); 89:6, 89:13-16, 89:22, 89:28 (رب); 89:22 (الملك)
- Synthesis: Title, central person, organizational support, and command form a coherent hierarchy scene.

#### Subchannel C. Idol, Tyrant, and Misplaced Worship
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A tyrannical leader or idol receives submission that properly belongs to a lord.
- Active motifs: idol or leader of error `ط غ و:B003/m01`; tyrant `ط غ و:B004/m01`; worship `ع ب د:B003/m01`; lordship `ر ب ب:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:11 (طغوا); 89:29 (عبادي); 89:6, 89:13-16, 89:22, 89:28 (رب)
- Synthesis: Political and ritual allegiance collapse into one scene when coercive leadership becomes an object of worship.

#### Subchannel D. Transgression Multiplying Corruption
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: Agents exceed limits within settled lands and multiply corrupt outcomes.
- Active motifs: transgression `ط غ ي:B001/m01`; excess beyond bounds `ط غ و:B001/m01`; corruption `ف س د:B001/m01`; multiplication `ك ث ر:B001/m01`; land or town `ب ل د:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:11-12 (طغوا في البلاد، أكثروا فيها الفساد)
- Synthesis: The surface sequence is causal: boundary-crossing action, repeated expansion, and environmental or social corruption.

#### Subchannel E. Watch, Lookout, and Prepared Trap
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A watcher occupies a prepared station and waits for an approaching target.
- Active motifs: watch `ر ص د:B001/m01`; prepared lookout `ر ص د:B002/m01`; trap or station `ر ص د:B003/m01`; approach or confrontation `ء ر ض:B007/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:14 (المرصاد); 89:21 (الأرض)
- Synthesis: Observer, station, preparation, and target movement make surveillance a functional scene rather than an abstract attribute.

#### Subchannel F. Scourge and Exemplary Punishment
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A whip or overwhelming force strikes the offender and leaves a public deterrent example.
- Active motifs: whip `س و ط:B002/m01`; punishment `ع ذ ب:B005/m01`; overwhelming sanction `ط غ و:B005/m01`; exemplary punishment `م ث ل:B002/m01`; humiliation `ه و ن:B003/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:13 (سوط عذاب); 89:11 (طغوا); 89:8 (مثلها); 89:16 (أهانن)
- Synthesis: Instrument, strike, suffering, lowered status, and deterrent memory form a complete punitive process.

#### Subchannel G. Unmatched Binding and Punishment
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: On the decisive day, no other agent matches the punishment or binding imposed.
- Active motifs: exhaustive “no one” `ء ح د:B002/m01`; punishment `ع ذ ب:B005/m01`; binding `و ث ق:B003/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:25-26 (لا يعذب عذابه أحد، لا يوثق وثاقه أحد)
- Synthesis: Repeated negation isolates one unmatched agent and pairs two sanctions: pain imposed and movement secured.

### 15. Belonging, Lineage, and Social Boundary
- Semantic invariant: Groups maintain identity by descent, shared residence, collective presence, naming, and controlled inclusion.
- Surface relation: indirect; 89:6-11 names peoples, places, lands, and monumental group identities.
- Surprising reach: Lineage extends into crowds, tent households, intimate insiders, strangers, mountains, and personal names.

#### Subchannel A. Tribe, Clan, and Lineage
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A named lineage persists as a clan whose members recognize common descent.
- Active motifs: tribe `ب ل و:B008/m01`; tribe `ب ل ي:B007/m01`; clan `ع ش ر:B013/m01`; tribal line `ح ي ي:B010/m01`; ʿĀd as lineage-name `ع و د:B012/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:15-16 (ابتلاه); 89:2 (عشر); 89:24 (حياتي); 89:6 (عاد)
- Synthesis: Descent, collective name, and affiliated members form a kinship scene behind the surface naming of ʿĀd.

#### Subchannel B. Multitude, Crowd, and Row
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Many people gather and then take ordered places as a visible collective.
- Active motifs: tribal gathering `ج م م:B003/m01`; mass of people `ج ن ن:B013/m01`; large groups `ر ب ب:B004/m01`; row `ص ف ف:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:20 (جما); 89:30 (جنتي); 89:6, 89:13-16, 89:22, 89:28 (رب); 89:22 (صفا)
- Synthesis: The group moves from demographic mass to organized public presence.

#### Subchannel C. Tent Household and Encampment
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A mobile household occupies a tented place held together by a main support and covenant.
- Active motifs: tent people `ع م د:B004/m01`; household `س ك ن:B003/m01`; dwelling place `س ك ن:B002/m01`; covenant `ر ب ب:B011/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:7 (العماد); 89:18 (المسكين); 89:6, 89:13-16, 89:22, 89:28 (رب)
- Synthesis: Shelter, inhabitants, central support, and social bond form one encampment scene.

#### Subchannel D. Intimate, Stranger, and Interloper
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A social boundary distinguishes the familiar intimate from the stranger or inserted outsider.
- Active motifs: intimate companion `ء ن س:B006/m01`; familiarity `ء ن س:B003/m01`; stranger `ء ر ض:B004/m01`; insider or interloper `د خ ل:B005/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:15, 89:23 (الإنسان); 89:21 (الأرض); 89:29-30 (ادخلي)
- Synthesis: Familiarity grants interior status; foreignness and insertion test whether the social boundary will admit or repel a newcomer.

#### Subchannel E. Names Across Person, Tribe, and Mountain
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A proper name transfers among person, lineage, place, and landmark to preserve affiliation.
- Active motifs: Iram or tribal place-name `ء ر م:B003/m01`; ʿĀd as name `ع و د:B012/m01`; Uhud as mountain-name `ء ح د:B006/m01`; personal or mountain name `ر ض و:B007/m01`; Layla as personal name `ل ي ل:B004/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:7 (إرم); 89:6 (عاد); 89:25-26 (أحد); 89:28 (راضية مرضية); 89:2, 89:4 (ليل)
- Synthesis: Naming maps social identity onto geography, allowing lineage memory to persist in mountains, settlements, and persons.

### 16. Human and Unseen Agency
- Semantic invariant: Human identity is defined in relation to unseen beings, affliction, and ultimate allegiance.
- Surface relation: indirect; 89:15, 89:23, 89:25-30 anchor the human, unique agent, servants, and garden.
- Surprising reach: The lexical field links mankind and jinn, spirit-induced madness, and the contrast between oneness and idol worship.

#### Subchannel A. Mankind and Jinn
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Human inhabitants and concealed jinn occupy complementary visible and unseen roles.
- Active motifs: humans `ء ن س:B001/m01`; jinn `ج ن ن:B005/m01`; hiddenness `ج ن ن:B001/m01`; inhabited place `ب ل د:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:15, 89:23 (الإنسان); 89:30 (جنتي); 89:8, 89:11 (البلاد)
- Synthesis: The human/jinn contrast is grounded in visibility versus concealment within a shared inhabited world.

#### Subchannel B. Spirit Affliction and Madness
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: An unseen familiar or touch overwhelms human reason and produces derangement.
- Active motifs: jinn familiar `ر ء ي:B008/m01`; jinn touch `ل م م:B004/m01`; madness `ج ن ن:B006/m01`; earth-spirit derangement `ء ر ض:B012/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:6 (تر); 89:19 (لما); 89:30 (جنتي); 89:21 (الأرض)
- Synthesis: External unseen agent, contact, displaced reason, and altered state form a coherent affliction scene.

#### Subchannel C. Oneness Against Idol Allegiance
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Exclusive oneness rejects submission to an idol or misleading ruler.
- Active motifs: absolute oneness `ء ح د:B001/m01`; idol or leader of error `ط غ و:B003/m01`; worship `ع ب د:B003/m01`; true lordship `ر ب ب:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:25-26 (أحد); 89:11 (طغوا); 89:29 (عبادي); 89:6, 89:13-16, 89:22, 89:28 (رب)
- Synthesis: Singular rightful authority and counterfeit allegiance are placed in direct structural opposition.

### 17. Delay, Exertion, and Return
- Semantic invariant: Movement is shaped by waiting, pace, fatigue, approach, reversal, and recovery.
- Surface relation: direct; 89:4, 89:22-24, and 89:28-30 provide night travel, coming, advancing, return, and entry.
- Surprising reach: Motion extends into animal gait, slack bowstrings, delayed maturity, visiting the sick, and convalescence.

#### Subchannel A. Waiting and Delay
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: An expected arrival is held back until a delayed time matures.
- Active motifs: delay `ء ل ي:B013/m01`; delayed time `ء ن ي:B001/m01`; postponement `ي ت م:B004/m01`; interval `و ت ر:B004/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:28 (إلى); 89:23 (أنى); 89:17 (اليتيم); 89:3 (الوتر)
- Synthesis: Delay, waiting participant, and due interval form a single temporal action scene.

#### Subchannel B. Fatigue, Pace, and Slackening
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A traveler or animal maintains pace, then slackens through fatigue or neglect.
- Active motifs: animal gait `ر ج ع:B008/m01`; sustained pace `ص ب ب:B010/m01`; slackening `و ت ر:B006/m01`; negligence `ي ت م:B003/m01`; fatigue or breakdown `ع ب د:B011/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:28 (ارجعي); 89:13 (صب); 89:3 (الوتر); 89:17 (اليتيم); 89:29 (عبادي)
- Synthesis: Motion, pace-control, loss of tension, and stopping outcome define a travel-fatigue process.

#### Subchannel C. Approach and Confrontation
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: An agent advances toward another, arrives, and may compel an encounter.
- Active motifs: confrontation `ء ر ض:B007/m01`; coming `ج ي ء:B001/m01`; compelled arrival `ج ي ء:B005/m01`; advance `ق د م:B004/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:21 (الأرض); 89:22-23 (جاء، جيء); 89:24 (قدمت)
- Synthesis: Direction, moving agent, target, arrival, and coercive outcome form a complete encounter scene.

#### Subchannel D. Return and Homecoming
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A traveler reverses course and reaches a familiar return-place.
- Active motifs: return `ر ج ع:B001/m01`; return `ع و د:B001/m01`; return-place `ع و د:B002/m01`; homecoming from travel `ق د م:B005/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:28 (ارجعي); 89:6 (عاد); 89:24 (قدمت)
- Synthesis: The same-root returns converge on route reversal, destination, and completed arrival.

#### Subchannel E. Convalescence and Visiting the Sick
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Illness gives way to recovery, supported by return visits and renewed function.
- Active motifs: recovery `ب ل ل:B003/m01`; recovery after illness `ر ج ع:B014/m01`; restored condition `م ث ل:B012/m01`; visiting the sick `ع و د:B005/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:17 (بل); 89:28 (ارجعي); 89:8 (مثلها); 89:6 (عاد)
- Synthesis: Patient state, recuperative change, visitor, and restored outcome make a coherent convalescence scene.

### 18. Residence, Refuge, and Tranquility
- Semantic invariant: A place becomes home when it affords stable entry, concealment, nourishment, and calm.
- Surface relation: direct; 89:27-30 moves from tranquility and return to entry among servants and into the garden.
- Surprising reach: Secure belonging extends into bedding, pasture-fixed residence, ship stabilization, and burial as final lodging.

#### Subchannel A. Settlement and Sustained Residence
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A resident remains in a suitable place because shelter and sustenance remove the need to depart.
- Active motifs: residing `ب ل د:B009/m01`; staying `ر ب ب:B007/m01`; residence `س ك ن:B002/m01`; suitable dwelling `ن ع م:B011/m01`; sustaining pasture `س ك ن:B010/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:8, 89:11 (البلاد); 89:6, 89:13-16, 89:22, 89:28 (رب); 89:18 (المسكين); 89:15 (نعمه)
- Synthesis: Place, inhabitant, continued stay, and local provision form a stable settlement scene.

#### Subchannel B. Shelter, Bedding, and Concealed Refuge
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A person withdraws into a concealed place furnished for rest.
- Active motifs: hiding place `ج ن ن:B017/m01`; chamber `ح ج ر:B004/m01`; bedding or coverlet `م ث ل:B007/m01`; thick rug `ء ر ض:B005/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:30 (جنتي); 89:5 (حجر); 89:8 (مثلها); 89:21 (الأرض)
- Synthesis: Boundary, hidden interior, floor covering, and resting surface produce a complete refuge scene.

#### Subchannel C. Stillness, Comfort, and Trust
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Movement ceases as the resident becomes calm, comfortable, and assured.
- Active motifs: stillness `س ك ن:B001/m01`; comfort `س ك ن:B004/m01`; tranquility `ط م ء ن:B001/m01`; calm `ط م ن:B001/m01`; trust `و ث ق:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:18 (المسكين); 89:27 (المطمئنة); 89:26 (وثاقه)
- Synthesis: The scene traces an embodied transition from motion to repose and then to relational assurance.

#### Subchannel D. Entry Into Home
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A returning participant crosses a boundary and joins an inhabited interior.
- Active motifs: entry `د خ ل:B001/m01`; dwelling `س ك ن:B002/m01`; return-place `ع و د:B002/m01`; intimate belonging `ء ن س:B006/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:29-30 (ادخلي); 89:18 (المسكين); 89:6 (عاد); 89:15, 89:23 (الإنسان)
- Synthesis: Approach, threshold crossing, interior, and recognized membership form a domestic entry scene.

#### Subchannel E. The Tranquil Soul's Final Entry
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: The tranquil soul returns accepted, joins the servants, and enters the garden.
- Active motifs: soul `ن ف س:B011/m01`; tranquility `ط م ن:B001/m01`; return `ر ج ع:B001/m01`; mutual acceptance `ر ض و:B003/m01`; entry `د خ ل:B001/m01`; worshiping servants `ع ب د:B003/m01`; garden `ج ن ن:B003/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:27-30 (النفس المطمئنة، ارجعي، راضية مرضية، ادخلي في عبادي، ادخلي جنتي)
- Synthesis: State, summons, movement, reciprocal approval, community, and destination form the surah's most complete belonging scene.

#### Subchannel F. Burial as Final Lodging
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A body is placed beneath earth in a grave and concealed as in a final dwelling.
- Active motifs: burial `ج ن ن:B009/m01`; burying with earth `د ك ك:B003/m01`; grave-place `ب ل د:B001/m02`; lap-like enclosure `ح ج ر:B005/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:30 (جنتي); 89:21 (دكت); 89:8, 89:11 (البلاد); 89:5 (حجر)
- Synthesis: Excavated place, body, earthen covering, and concealment make burial a terminal residence scene.

### 19. Crafted Instruments and Material Assemblies
- Semantic invariant: Raw material is shaped, joined, tensioned, or folded into a tool with a specific function.
- Surface relation: indirect; 89:7, 89:9-10, 89:13, 89:22, and 89:26 anchor supports, cutting, pegs, whip, alignment, and binding.
- Surprising reach: The artifact system includes adzes, bowstrings, training targets, shields, rudders, linen pleats, gambling kits, necklaces, and incense.

#### Subchannel A. Wood, Stick, and Adze
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Timber is cut or carved with an adze and fashioned into a stick, bat, or support.
- Active motifs: wood or stick `ع و د:B010/m01`; bat or stick `ق و ل:B008/m01`; adze `ق د م:B008/m01`; cutting `ج و ب:B001/m01`; column `ع م د:B003/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:6 (عاد); 89:15-16, 89:24 (يقول); 89:24 (قدمت); 89:9 (جابوا); 89:7 (العماد)
- Synthesis: Material, cutting tool, shaping action, and finished implements form a woodworking scene.

#### Subchannel B. Whip, Strap, and Knife
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A handled instrument carries a flexible lash or cutting edge and leaves a mark on the body.
- Active motifs: whip `س و ط:B002/m01`; hanging whip-tip or strap `ع ذ ب:B006/m01`; knife `س ك ن:B007/m01`; bodily mark `ب ل د:B006/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:13 (سوط عذاب); 89:18 (المسكين); 89:8, 89:11 (البلاد)
- Synthesis: Handle, flexible extension or blade, action on flesh, and resulting mark form one instrument-of-injury scene.

#### Subchannel C. Bowstring, Peg, and Training Target
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A string is tensioned between fixed points and aimed at a circular target.
- Active motifs: bowstring `و ت ر:B007/m01`; fixed peg `و ت د:B001/m01`; circular training mark `و ت ر:B008/m01`; circular eye-like mark `ح ج ر:B006/m03`; hunting implement `ط ع م:B006/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:3 (الوتر); 89:10 (الأوتاد); 89:5 (حجر); 89:18 (طعام)
- Synthesis: Anchor, tension, projectile function, and target complete an archery and marksmanship mechanism.

#### Subchannel D. Shield and Armor
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A shaped covering receives impact while protecting the body behind it.
- Active motifs: shield `ج ن ن:B008/m01`; shield or protective clothing `ج و ب:B005/m01`; smooth unprojecting surface `ج م م:B006/m01`; binding firmness `و ث ق:B002/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:30 (جنتي); 89:9 (جابوا); 89:20 (جما); 89:26 (وثاقه)
- Synthesis: Covering, body, impact-facing surface, and reinforcement form a defensive artifact scene.

#### Subchannel E. Rudder and Stabilized Vessel
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A rudder at the stern controls a vessel and dampens its oscillation.
- Active motifs: ship rudder `س ك ن:B008/m01`; calming control `ط م ن:B001/m01`; compelled direction `ج ي ء:B005/m01`; firm reliance `و ث ق:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:18 (المسكين); 89:27 (المطمئنة); 89:22-23 (جاء، جيء); 89:26 (وثاقه)
- Synthesis: Vessel, steering component, controlling action, and stabilized outcome form a complete navigational mechanism.

#### Subchannel F. Linen, Damp Folding, and Tanning
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Cloth or hide is dampened, folded into pleats, treated by measured application, and allowed to wear.
- Active motifs: linen `ر ز ق:B007/m01`; damp folding `ب ل ل:B007/m01`; first fold or pleat `ق س م:B007/m01`; tanning dose `ن ف س:B007/m01`; wear of material `خ ل ق:B009/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:16 (رزقه); 89:17 (بل); 89:5 (قسم); 89:27 (النفس); 89:8 (يخلق)
- Synthesis: Material, moisture, repeated fold, measured treatment, and aging outcome form a textile-and-hide preparation scene.

#### Subchannel G. Gaming Arrows, Lot, and Container
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Marked arrows or lots are stored together, drawn, and used to allocate an outcome.
- Active motifs: arrow-lot container `ر ب ب:B010/m01`; gambling lot `ن ف س:B016/m01`; palm basket `د خ ل:B010/m01`; draw from a quiver `ر ج ع:B010/m01`; odd or single result `و ت ر:B001/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:6, 89:13-16, 89:22, 89:28 (رب); 89:27 (النفس); 89:29-30 (ادخلي); 89:28 (ارجعي); 89:3 (الوتر)
- Synthesis: Container, marked pieces, random draw, and allocated result form a complete gaming-lot mechanism.

#### Subchannel H. Necklace, Incense, and Perfume Preparation
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Aromatic wood is ground or prepared while beads are joined into a worn ornament.
- Active motifs: necklace `ك ر م:B003/m01`; aromatic wood `ء ل ي:B014/m01`; incense wood `ع و د:B010/m02`; perfume `خ ل ق:B010/m01`; perfume mortar `ع ب د:B012/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:15, 89:17 (أكرم); 89:28 (إلى); 89:6 (عاد); 89:8 (يخلق); 89:29 (عبادي)
- Synthesis: The branch paths join two workshop products through material preparation: aromatic substance is ground, while ornament components are assembled for wear.

### 20. Damage, Decay, and Repair
- Semantic invariant: Bodies and materials deteriorate through wear, consumption, heat, or infection and may be restored through treatment.
- Surface relation: indirect; 89:12-13, 89:21, and 89:28 anchor corruption, punishment, earth, and return.
- Surprising reach: Moral corruption has concrete counterparts in frayed cloth, termite-eaten wood, rekindled embers, pus, and convalescent repair.

#### Subchannel A. Wear, Fraying, and Corrosion
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Repeated use thins a surface until fibers fray and material is consumed.
- Active motifs: wear `ب ل و:B001/m01`; wear `ب ل ي:B001/m01`; worn surface `خ ل ق:B009/m01`; corrosion `ء ك ل:B006/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:15-16 (ابتلاه); 89:8 (يخلق); 89:19 (أكل)
- Synthesis: Use, abrasion, thinning, and material loss form a single decay process across cloth, hide, and hard surface.

#### Subchannel B. Termite Gnawing Through Wood
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A termite enters timber and consumes it until the structure is hollowed or eradicated.
- Active motifs: termite `ء ر ض:B010/m01`; corrosion or gnawing `ء ك ل:B006/m01`; total consumption `ء ر م:B006/m01`; wood `ع و د:B010/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:21 (الأرض); 89:19 (أكل); 89:7 (إرم); 89:6 (عاد)
- Synthesis: Agent, material, repeated gnawing, and structural loss make a specific biological decay scene.

#### Subchannel C. Fire, Sparks, and Rekindled Embers
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Sparks ignite material, fire consumes it, and buried embers can be stirred back into flame.
- Active motifs: fire consuming `ء ك ل:B005/m01`; sparks `ح ب ب:B011/m01`; stirring inherited embers `و ر ث:B005/m01`; burial or covering `د ك ك:B003/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:19 (أكل); 89:20 (حبا); 89:19 (التراث); 89:21 (دكت)
- Synthesis: Ignition, consumption, concealment, and rekindling form a cyclical fire process.

#### Subchannel D. Wound, Pus, and Suppuration
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A wound or sore collects pus, opens, and discharges infected material.
- Active motifs: festering sore `ء ر ض:B011/m01`; pus `ج ي ء:B003/m01`; suppurating wound `ج ي ء:B006/m01`; aperture `ج و ب:B004/m01`; blood or discharge `ص ب ب:B006/m02`
- Ayah anchors: 89:21 (الأرض); 89:22-23 (جاء، جيء); 89:9 (جابوا); 89:13 (صب)
- Synthesis: Lesion, collected material, opening, and discharge form a complete pathology scene.

#### Subchannel E. Nurture, Treatment, and Restoration
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A damaged body or object is treated, repaired, and returned to sound condition.
- Active motifs: repair and nurture `ر ب ب:B002/m01`; medicinal preparation `ر ب ب:B006/m02`; recovery `ب ل ل:B003/m01`; restoration `م ث ل:B012/m01`; return to health `ر ج ع:B014/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:6, 89:13-16, 89:22, 89:28 (رب); 89:17 (بل); 89:8 (مثلها); 89:28 (ارجعي)
- Synthesis: Care, remedy, patient or object, and restored outcome reverse the deterioration scenes without collapsing into them.

## Standalone Subchannels

### S1. Raised Cairn as Marker
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Stones are raised and joined into a visible cairn that marks a place or route.
- Active motifs: stone marker `ء ر م:B002/m01`; boulder `ص خ ر:B001/m01`; raised joined summit `ء ر م:B001/m01`; visible standard `ر ء ي:B011/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:7 (إرم); 89:9 (الصخر); 89:6 (تر)
- Synthesis: Material, stacking action, elevation, and visual function form a complete landmark scene.

### S2. Banner and Vanguard
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A visible banner precedes a moving group and provides orientation.
- Active motifs: banner `ر ء ي:B011/m01`; foremost guide `م ل ك:B008/m02`; vanguard `ق د م:B007/m01`; elevated support `ع م د:B005/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:6 (تر); 89:22 (الملك); 89:24 (قدمت); 89:7 (العماد)
- Synthesis: Signal, bearer, forward position, and following group define a scene of guided collective movement.

### S3. Echo, Clang, and Counted Bray
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A sharp sound strikes a hard surface, reverberates, and may be recognized or counted by repeated calls.
- Active motifs: echo `ر ج ع:B007/m01`; metal or stone clang `ص خ ر:B004/m01`; nightingale-like sound `ب ل ل:B010/m01`; counted brays `ع ش ر:B008/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:28 (ارجعي); 89:9 (الصخر); 89:17 (بل); 89:2 (عشر)
- Synthesis: Sound source, hard resonator, return wave, and repeated count form a coherent acoustic scene.

### S4. Sudden Collective Eruption
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A dense crowd or event breaks out suddenly with overwhelming noise.
- Active motifs: sudden mass event `ف ج ر:B003/m01`; crowd `د ك ك:B008/m01`; overwhelming noise `ط غ و:B008/m01`; gathering `ص ف ف:B007/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:1 (الفجر); 89:21 (دكا); 89:11 (طغوا); 89:22 (صفا)
- Synthesis: Sudden onset, dense participants, clamor, and collective assembly define an eruption scene distinct from gradual gathering.

### S5. False Accusation and Surrender of a Person
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A false claim is circulated and used to hand a person over to another party.
- Active motifs: allegation or handing over a person `ء ك ل:B010/m01`; false attribution `ق و ل:B005/m01`; rumor `ق و ل:B007/m01`; compelled presentation `ج ي ء:B004/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:19 (أكل); 89:15-16, 89:24 (يقول); 89:22-23 (جاء، جيء)
- Synthesis: Accusation, verbal fabrication, circulation, and delivery of the accused form a specific coercive discourse scene.

### S6. Pounding in a Mortar
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Material is placed in a mortar and repeatedly pounded into a usable preparation.
- Active motifs: mortar `ه و ن:B004/m01`; pounding `د ك ك:B001/m01`; vessel `ء ن ي:B004/m01`; medicinal paste `ر ب ب:B006/m02`
- Ayah anchors: 89:16 (أهانن); 89:21 (دكا); 89:23 (أنى); 89:6, 89:13-16, 89:22, 89:28 (رب)
- Synthesis: Container, material, repeated impact, and processed outcome define a compact preparation mechanism.

### S7. Returned Waste and Reuse
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Discarded material returns from a prior use or bodily process and is gathered for reuse or disposal.
- Active motifs: returned waste or material `ر ج ع:B015/m01`; hidden residue or defect `د خ ل:B004/m01`; gathered remainder `ص ب ب:B003/m01`
- Ayah anchors: 89:28 (ارجعي); 89:29-30 (ادخلي); 89:13 (صب)
- Synthesis: Residue, return path, and collection form a small but complete recovery-of-material scene.
